人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)感染相关上皮-间质转化:在肿瘤发生及HPV阳性肿瘤治疗中的意义

Human Papillomavirus (HPV) Infection-Associated Epithelial-to-Mesenchymal Transition: Implication in Tumorigenesis and Pathogenesis of HPV-Positive Cancers
作者： Alexander E. Kabakov, Olga N. Matchuk, Anna V. Khokhlova, Andrey D. Kaprin, Матчук Ольга Николаевна, А. Д. Каприн
摘要中文翻译上皮间质转化(EMT)是一个程序化的可逆过程,允许上皮细胞暂时获得具有增强的迁移和侵袭能力的间充质细胞表型。类似的表型调节也可以发生在癌细胞中,癌细胞在经历 EMT 后,表现出癌症干细胞样特性并主动迁移,导致肿瘤侵袭和转移。这篇结构化叙述性综述重点关注 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 和 EMT 之间的分子串扰,这种串扰发生在 HPV 感染的人类上皮和不同部位(宫颈、头颈、结直肠、肺癌等)的 HPV 阳性癌症中。这里讨论的主要问题是:(i)HPV驱动的EMT如何促进HPV感染的上皮细胞的恶性转化,(ii)人类癌症中HPV阳性和EMT之间可能存在什么机制联系,以及(iii)各种HPV阳性人类肿瘤中的EMT如何影响其发病机制、治疗模式和结果。HPV感染与EMT程序之间的关系被描述为由外源性和内源性刺激触发的复杂的多组分机制,涉及特定的配体-受体相互作用、膜通道、转录因子、信号通路、表观遗传调节因子等。该综述还讨论了EMT相关生物标志物和分子靶点在HPV诱导的癌症的预防、预测和治疗中的一些潜在应用。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述探讨了上皮间质转化 (EMT) 在 HPV 感染中的作用及其对肿瘤发生和 HPV 阳性癌症的 发病机制 的影响。它具体阐述了 HPV 驱动的 EMT 如何促进恶性转化、人类癌症中 HPV 阳性与 EMT 之间的机制联系,以及 EMT 对 治疗 结果和各种 HPV 阳性肿瘤的 发病机制 的影响。
证据基础与方法
该综述是一个结构化的叙述性综合,重点关注 HPV 和 EMT 之间的分子相互作用。它不报告特定的数据库、检索日期或荟萃分析程序。该综述方法基于对HPV和EMT相关文献的综合分析,重点关注分子串扰及其在癌症进展中的作用。
共识与结论
该综述的结论是,HPV 感染与人类上皮细胞的 EMT 相关,这有助于 HPV 阳性癌症的发展。它强调了复杂的多组分机制,涉及配体-受体相互作用、转录因子、信号通路和将 HPV 感染与 EMT 联系起来的表观遗传调节因子。研究结果表明EMT在HPV感染的上皮细胞的恶性转化中发挥着关键作用。
争议与缺口
该综述指出了在了解 HPV 触发 EMT 的精确机制及其在不同 HPV 阳性癌症中的作用方面存在的差距。它还指出,虽然 EMT 与肿瘤侵袭和转移有关,但 EMT 对 治疗 结果和耐药性的确切贡献仍不清楚。这些差距突出表明需要进一步研究分子途径及其临床相关性。
科研与实践启示
该综述表明,EMT 相关生物标志物和分子靶点对于 HPV 诱发的癌症的预防、预测和 治疗 可能有价值。它强调了在临床环境中研究这些生物标志物以改善 HPV 阳性恶性肿瘤的早期检测和治疗策略的重要性。

Human Papillomavirus (HPV) Infection-Associated Epithelial-to-Mesenchymal Transition: Implication in Tumorigenesis and Pathogenesis of HPV-Positive Cancers

Authors: Alexander E. Kabakov, Olga N. Matchuk, Anna V. Khokhlova, Andrey D. Kaprin, Матчук Ольга Николаевна, А. Д. Каприн
Original abstract (English)
Epithelial-to-mesenchymal transition (EMT) is a programmed and reversible process that allows epithelial cells to temporarily acquire a mesenchymal cell phenotype with increased migratory and invasive capacity. Analogous phenotypic modulation can also occur in carcinoma cells, which, after undergoing EMT, exhibit cancer stem cell-like properties and actively migrate, leading to tumor invasion and metastases. This structured narrative review focuses on the molecular crosstalk between human papillomavirus (HPV) and EMT, which occurs in HPV-infected human epithelium and in HPV-positive cancers of different location (cervical, head and neck, colorectal, lung, and others). The main questions addressed here are: (i) how HPV-driven EMT may contribute to the malignant transformation of HPV-infected epithelial cells, (ii) what mechanistic links may exist between the HPV positivity and EMT in human cancers, and (iii) how EMT in various HPV-positive human tumors may influence their pathogenesis, treatment modalities and outcomes. The relationship between HPV infection and EMT program is delineated as a complex multicomponent mechanism triggered by exogenous and endogenous stimuli and involving specific ligand-receptor interactions, membrane channels, transcription factors, signaling pathways, epigenetic regulators, etc. The review also discusses some of the potential applications of EMT-related biomarkers and molecular targets in the prevention, prediction and treatment of HPV-induced cancers.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review explores the role of epithelial-to-mesenchymal transition (EMT) in HPV infection and its implications for tumorigenesis and pathogenesis of HPV-positive cancers. It specifically addresses how HPV-driven EMT contributes to malignant transformation, the mechanistic links between HPV positivity and EMT in human cancers, and the influence of EMT on treatment outcomes and pathogenesis of various HPV-positive tumors.
Evidence base and method
The review is a structured narrative synthesis focusing on the molecular interactions between HPV and EMT. It does not report specific databases, search dates, or meta-analysis procedures. The review method is based on a comprehensive analysis of the literature related to HPV and EMT, with an emphasis on molecular crosstalk and its role in cancer progression.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that HPV infection is associated with EMT in human epithelial cells, which contributes to the development of HPV-positive cancers. It highlights the complex multicomponent mechanism involving ligand-receptor interactions, transcription factors, signaling pathways, and epigenetic regulators that link HPV infection to EMT. The findings suggest that EMT plays a critical role in the malignant transformation of HPV-infected epithelial cells.
Controversies and gaps
The review identifies gaps in understanding the precise mechanisms by which HPV triggers EMT and its role in different HPV-positive cancers. It also notes that while EMT is implicated in tumor invasion and metastasis, the exact contribution of EMT to treatment outcomes and resistance remains unclear. These gaps highlight the need for further research into the molecular pathways and their clinical relevance.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that EMT-related biomarkers and molecular targets could be valuable for the prevention, prediction, and treatment of HPV-induced cancers. It emphasizes the importance of investigating these biomarkers in clinical settings to improve early detection and therapeutic strategies for HPV-positive malignancies.

阴道微生物群感染持续存在 HPV 感染进展 宫颈病变进展:UMA REVISÕO DA LITERATURA 阴道微生物群对 HPV 感染持续性和宫颈病变进展的影响:文献综述

INFLUÊNCIA DA MICROBIOTA VAGINAL NA PERSISTÊNCIA DA INFECÇÃO PELO HPV E NA PROGRESSÃO DAS LESÕES CERVICAIS: UMA REVISÃO DA LITERATURA INFLUENCE OF VAGINAL MICROBIOTA ON HPV INFECTION PERSISTENCE AND CERVICAL LESION PROGRESSION: A LITERATURE REVIEW
作者： Mariana Veríssimo de Lima Correia, Ana Luiza dos Santos
摘要中文翻译人类乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 感染,尤其是宫颈癌,与宫颈内膜癌和结肠癌有关。病毒的持续存在主要涉及该过程,最近有证据表明阴道微生物群的组成与感染的演变有关。Esse artigobuscou analisar对阴道微生物群的影响,对HPV感染的持久性和宫颈进展的影响。实现基于 PubMed 和 Biblioteca Virtual em Saúde (BVS) 的文献修订,利用“微生物群”、“乳头瘤病毒感染”和“子宫颈发育不良”描述,并结合布尔​​操作符和。论坛选出了 25 项公共研究,这些研究都是免费的,并且是完整的文本。与宫颈阴道病相关的结果表明,乳杆菌和 HPV 持久性的特殊成分的变化,是与宫颈不同部位的关系的。观察厌氧细菌的变化和与当地免疫反应相关的良好代谢关系。结论是阴道微生物群与 HPV 持续存在的微环境相关,并与相关进展相关,其中包含了因果关系。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
人类乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 感染,尤其是宫颈癌,与宫颈内膜癌和结肠癌有关。Esse artigobuscou analisar对阴道微生物群的影响,对HPV感染的持久性和宫颈进展的影响。
证据基础与方法
实现基于 PubMed 和 Biblioteca Virtual em Saúde (BVS) 的文献修订,利用“微生物群”、“乳头瘤病毒感染”和“子宫颈发育不良”描述,并结合布尔​​操作符和。论坛选出了 25 项公共研究,这些研究都是免费的,并且是完整的文本。
共识与结论
与宫颈阴道病相关的结果表明,乳杆菌和 HPV 持久性的特殊成分的变化,是与宫颈不同部位的关系的。观察厌氧细菌的变化和与当地免疫反应相关的良好代谢关系。结论是阴道微生物群与 HPV 持续存在的微环境相关,并与相关进展相关,其中包含了因果关系。
争议与缺口
阴道微生物群与 HPV 持续存在之间存在因果关系,或与已建立的 HPV 的进展有关。存在关联原则的证据,需要进行辅助研究以确认原因和治疗效果。
科研与实践启示
有必要对引起HPV感染的原因和阴道微生物群的影响进行进一步的研究和宫颈癌的进展。Além disso 是一种与 HPV 感染预防和治疗相关的微生物阴道血清。

INFLUÊNCIA DA MICROBIOTA VAGINAL NA PERSISTÊNCIA DA INFECÇÃO PELO HPV E NA PROGRESSÃO DAS LESÕES CERVICAIS: UMA REVISÃO DA LITERATURA INFLUENCE OF VAGINAL MICROBIOTA ON HPV INFECTION PERSISTENCE AND CERVICAL LESION PROGRESSION: A LITERATURE REVIEW

Authors: Mariana Veríssimo de Lima Correia, Ana Luiza dos Santos
Original abstract (English)
A infecção pelo papilomavírus humano (HPV), especialmente pelos tipos de alto risco, está relacionada ao desenvolvimento de lesões intraepiteliais cervicais e ao câncer de colo do útero. Embora a persistência viral seja um dos principais fatores envolvidos nesse processo, evidências recentes indicam que a composição da microbiota vaginal pode estar relacionada à evolução da infecção. Esse artigo buscou analisar a influência da microbiota vaginal na persistência da infecção pelo HPV e na progressão das lesões cervicais. Foi realizada uma revisão da literatura nas bases PubMed e Biblioteca Virtual em Saúde (BVS), utilizando os descritores “Microbiota”, “Papillomavirus Infections” e “Uterine Cervical Dysplasia”, combinados pelo operador booleano AND. Foram selecionados 25 estudos publicados nos últimos cinco anos, disponíveis gratuitamente e em texto completo. Os resultados demonstraram associação entre disbiose cervicovaginal, alterações na composição de espécies de Lactobacillus e persistência do HPV, além de relações com diferentes graus de lesão cervical. Também foram observadas alterações em bactérias anaeróbias e em perfis metabólicos relacionados à resposta imune local. Conclui-se que a microbiota vaginal pode participar do microambiente associado à persistência do HPV e à progressão das lesões, embora a relação causal ainda não esteja completamente estabelecida.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
A infecção pelo papilomavírus humano (HPV), especialmente pelos tipos de alto risco, está relacionada ao desenvolvimento de lesões intraepiteliais cervicais e ao câncer de colo do útero. Esse artigo buscou analisar a influência da microbiota vaginal na persistência da infecção pelo HPV e na progressão das lesões cervicais.
Evidence base and method
Foi realizada uma revisão da literatura nas bases PubMed e Biblioteca Virtual em Saúde (BVS), utilizando os descritores “Microbiota”, “Papillomavirus Infections” e “Uterine Cervical Dysplasia”, combinados pelo operador booleano AND. Foram selecionados 25 estudos publicados nos últimos cinco anos, disponíveis gratuitamente e em texto completo.
Consensus and conclusions
Os resultados demonstraram associação entre disbiose cervicovaginal, alterações na composição de espécies de Lactobacillus e persistência do HPV, além de relações com diferentes graus de lesão cervical. Também foram observadas alterações em bactérias anaeróbias e em perfis metabólicos relacionados à resposta imune local. Conclui-se que a microbiota vaginal pode participar do microambiente associado à persistência do HPV e à progressão das lesões, embora a relação causal ainda não esteja completamente estabelecida.
Controversies and gaps
A relação causal entre a microbiota vaginal e a persistência do HPV ou progressão das lesões ainda não está completamente estabelecida. A evidência apresentada é principalmente associativa, e há necessidade de estudos adicionais para confirmar mecanismos causais e efetos terapêuticos.
Research and practice implications
É necessário realizar estudos adicionais para investigar mecanismos causais e efetos terapêuticos da microbiota vaginal na infecção pelo HPV e progressão das lesões cervicais. Além disso, a microbiota vaginal pode ser um fator relevante para estratégias de prevenção e tratamento de infecções pelo HPV.

人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 女性的性健康和福祉:范围界定审查

Sexual Health and Well-Being in Women with Human Papillomavirus (HPV): A Scoping Review
作者： Ana Margarida Carriço de Seixas, Diana Patrícia Pinto Coelho, Priscila A Vasconcelos, Ana Luísa Quinta-Gomes
摘要中文翻译本次范围界定审查旨在绘制 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 感染对女性性健康和性福祉影响的研究。尽管 HPV 是全球最流行的性传播感染之一,并且大多数感染都是短暂的且临床良性,但越来越多的证据表明 HPV 诊断、后续程序和 治疗 可能对女性产生重大的情感、心理社会、关系和性后果。该综述以以下总体研究问题为指导:HPV 感染女性在整个临床轨迹(筛查、诊断、治疗 和随访)中与情感、心理社会、关系和性功能结果相关的性健康和福祉的证据的性质是什么?遵循 Joanna Briggs Institute (JBI) 的范围审查方法和 PRISMA-ScR 报告指南,本次审查包括针对被诊断患有宫颈、阴道或外阴 HPV 感染的成年女性的实证定量、定性和混合方法研究。证据将涵盖多个领域,包括情绪反应(例如焦虑、恐惧、羞耻、痛苦)、心理社会因素(例如耻辱、健康素养、自我形象、应对)、关系和沟通过程、性功能(例如欲望、性唤起、性高潮、疼痛、满意度)以及与 HPV 临床路径不同阶段相关的经历。本次综述的预期结果是对当前知识进行全面概述,确定文献中的模式和差距,并支持开发更综合的生物心理社会方法来治疗 HPV。这些发现可能有助于改善 HPV 感染女性的临床沟通、社会心理支持、性健康干预以及以患者为中心的护理。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本次范围界定审查旨在绘制 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 感染对女性性健康和性福祉影响的研究。
证据基础与方法
遵循 Joanna Briggs Institute (JBI) 的范围审查方法和 PRISMA-ScR 报告指南,本次审查包括针对被诊断患有宫颈、阴道或外阴 HPV 感染的成年女性的实证定量、定性和混合方法研究。
共识与结论
证据将涵盖多个领域,包括情绪反应(例如焦虑、恐惧、羞耻、痛苦)、心理社会因素(例如耻辱、健康素养、自我形象、应对)、关系和沟通过程、性功能(例如欲望、性唤起、性高潮、疼痛、满意度)以及与 HPV 临床路径不同阶段相关的经历。
争议与缺口
尽管 HPV 是全球最流行的性传播感染之一,并且大多数感染都是短暂的且临床良性,但越来越多的证据表明 HPV 诊断、后续程序和 治疗 可能对女性产生重大的情感、心理社会、关系和性后果。
科研与实践启示
这些发现可能有助于改善 HPV 感染女性的临床沟通、社会心理支持、性健康干预以及以患者为中心的护理。

Sexual Health and Well-Being in Women with Human Papillomavirus (HPV): A Scoping Review

Authors: Ana Margarida Carriço de Seixas, Diana Patrícia Pinto Coelho, Priscila A Vasconcelos, Ana Luísa Quinta-Gomes
Original abstract (English)
This scoping review aims to map the research on the impact of Human Papillomavirus (HPV) infection on women's sexual health and sexual well-being. Although HPV is one of the most prevalent sexually transmitted infections worldwide and that most infections are transient and clinically benign, growing evidence suggests that HPV diagnosis, follow-up procedures, and treatment may have significant emotional, psychosocial, relational, and sexual consequences for women. The review is guided by the following overarching research question: What is the nature of the evidence on the sexual health and well-being of women with HPV infection across its clinical trajectory (screening, diagnosis, treatment, and follow-up), in relation to emotional, psychosocial, relational, and sexual functioning outcomes? Following the Joanna Briggs Institute (JBI) methodology for scoping reviews and the PRISMA-ScR reporting guidelines, this review includes empirical quantitative, qualitative, and mixed-methods studies involving adult women diagnosed with cervical, vaginal, or vulvar HPV infection. Evidence will be mapped across several domains, including emotional responses (e.g., anxiety, fear, shame, distress), psychosocial factors (e.g., stigma, health literacy, self-image, coping), relationship and communication processes, sexual functioning (e.g., desire, arousal, orgasm, pain, satisfaction), and experiences associated with different stages of the HPV clinical pathway. The expected outcomes of this review are to provide a comprehensive overview of current knowledge, identify patterns and gaps in the literature, and support the development of more integrated biopsychosocial approaches to HPV care. The findings may contribute to improving clinical communication, psychosocial support, sexual health interventions, and patient-centred care for women living with HPV.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This scoping review aims to map the research on the impact of Human Papillomavirus (HPV) infection on women's sexual health and sexual well-being.
Evidence base and method
Following the Joanna Briggs Institute (JBI) methodology for scoping reviews and the PRISMA-ScR reporting guidelines, this review includes empirical quantitative, qualitative, and mixed-methods studies involving adult women diagnosed with cervical, vaginal, or vulvar HPV infection.
Consensus and conclusions
Evidence will be mapped across several domains, including emotional responses (e.g., anxiety, fear, shame, distress), psychosocial factors (e.g., stigma, health literacy, self-image, coping), relationship and communication processes, sexual functioning (e.g., desire, arousal, orgasm, pain, satisfaction), and experiences associated with different stages of the HPV clinical pathway.
Controversies and gaps
Although HPV is one of the most prevalent sexually transmitted infections worldwide and that most infections are transient and clinically benign, growing evidence suggests that HPV diagnosis, follow-up procedures, and treatment may have significant emotional, psychosocial, relational, and sexual consequences for women.
Research and practice implications
The findings may contribute to improving clinical communication, psychosocial support, sexual health interventions, and patient-centred care for women living with HPV.

宫颈癌 中的泛素特异性蛋白酶和免疫微环境:机制证据、免疫治疗耐药性和治疗前景。

Ubiquitin-specific proteases and the immune microenvironment in cervical cancer: mechanistic evidence, immunotherapy resistance, and therapeutic perspectives.
作者： Yan-Hua Zhang, Li-Ling Zhu, Lei Xia, Hui Cheng, Wei-Pei Zhu, Hong-You Wang
摘要中文翻译宫颈癌 仍然是全球主要的健康负担,耐药性限制了免疫检查点抑制剂 (ICIs) 的益处。泛素特异性蛋白酶 (USP) 调节蛋白质稳态,并可能将 人乳头瘤病毒 相关的肿瘤发生与免疫逃避联系起来。这篇叙述性综述评估了与 宫颈癌 相关的 USP 依赖性机制,将疾病特异性发现与其他肿瘤类型的证据和拟议的途径区分开来。宫颈癌 研究支持 USP7 相关的免疫检查点调节以及 USP14 依赖的 MCT4 稳定和乳酸输出,尽管后者的免疫后果仍然存在推断。将 USP 与调节性 T 细胞功能、STING 信号传导、抗原呈递和免疫抑制代谢联系起来的证据主要来自非宫颈实验系统。因此,所提出的 E6-USP7 和 E7-USP1 免疫途径需要直接验证。我们讨论小分子抑制剂、靶向蛋白质降解和稳定、组合策略和生物标志物开发。补偿性 USP 重编程 (CUR) 和提出的基于 USP 的肿瘤免疫微环境 (USP-TIME) 框架分别作为研究 治疗 相关适应以及 USP 谱与免疫结构之间关系的假设。治疗开发必须考虑到 USP 抑制也会损害抗原呈递或效应淋巴细胞功能的可能性。尽管 USP 指导的方法为进一步研究提供了理由,但它们在 宫颈癌 中的临床益处尚未得到证实。进展需要细胞类型解析的机制研究、免疫活性模型、经过验证的药效学分析和化合物特异性安全评估。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
宫颈癌 仍然是全球主要的健康负担,耐药性限制了免疫检查点抑制剂 (ICIs) 的益处。这篇叙述性综述评估了与 宫颈癌 相关的 USP 依赖性机制,将疾病特异性发现与其他肿瘤类型的证据和拟议的途径区分开来。
证据基础与方法
宫颈癌 研究支持 USP7 相关的免疫检查点调节以及 USP14 依赖的 MCT4 稳定和乳酸输出,尽管后者的免疫后果仍然存在推断。
共识与结论
将 USP 与调节性 T 细胞功能、STING 信号传导、抗原呈递和免疫抑制代谢联系起来的证据主要来自非宫颈实验系统。
争议与缺口
因此,所提出的 E6-USP7 和 E7-USP1 免疫途径需要直接验证。
科研与实践启示
补偿性 USP 重编程 (CUR) 和提出的基于 USP 的肿瘤免疫微环境 (USP-TIME) 框架分别作为研究 治疗 相关适应以及 USP 谱与免疫结构之间关系的假设。治疗开发必须考虑到 USP 抑制也会损害抗原呈递或效应淋巴细胞功能的可能性。

Ubiquitin-specific proteases and the immune microenvironment in cervical cancer: mechanistic evidence, immunotherapy resistance, and therapeutic perspectives.

Authors: Yan-Hua Zhang, Li-Ling Zhu, Lei Xia, Hui Cheng, Wei-Pei Zhu, Hong-You Wang
Original abstract (English)
Cervical cancer remains a major global health burden, and resistance limits the benefit of immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs). Ubiquitin-specific proteases (USPs) regulate protein homeostasis and may connect human papillomavirus-associated oncogenesis with immune evasion. This narrative review evaluates USP-dependent mechanisms relevant to cervical cancer, distinguishing disease-specific findings from evidence in other tumor types and proposed pathways. Cervical cancer studies support USP7-associated immune-checkpoint regulation and USP14-dependent MCT4 stabilization and lactate export, although the immune consequences of the latter remain inferred. Evidence linking USPs to regulatory T-cell function, STING signaling, antigen presentation, and immunosuppressive metabolism derives largely from non-cervical experimental systems. The proposed E6-USP7 and E7-USP1 immune pathways therefore require direct validation. We discuss small-molecule inhibitors, targeted protein degradation and stabilization, combination strategies, and biomarker development. Compensatory USP Reprogramming (CUR) and a proposed USP-based tumor immune microenvironment (USP-TIME) framework are presented as hypotheses for investigating treatment-associated adaptation and the relationship between USP profiles and immune architecture, respectively. Therapeutic development must account for the possibility that USP inhibition also impairs antigen presentation or effector lymphocyte function. Although USP-directed approaches provide a rationale for further investigation, their clinical benefit in cervical cancer remains unproven. Progress requires cell-type-resolved mechanistic studies, immunocompetent models, validated pharmacodynamic assays, and compound-specific safety assessment.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Cervical cancer remains a major global health burden, and resistance limits the benefit of immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs). This narrative review evaluates USP-dependent mechanisms relevant to cervical cancer, distinguishing disease-specific findings from evidence in other tumor types and proposed pathways.
Evidence base and method
Cervical cancer studies support USP7-associated immune-checkpoint regulation and USP14-dependent MCT4 stabilization and lactate export, although the immune consequences of the latter remain inferred.
Consensus and conclusions
Evidence linking USPs to regulatory T-cell function, STING signaling, antigen presentation, and immunosuppressive metabolism derives largely from non-cervical experimental systems.
Controversies and gaps
The proposed E6-USP7 and E7-USP1 immune pathways therefore require direct validation.
Research and practice implications
Compensatory USP Reprogramming (CUR) and a proposed USP-based tumor immune microenvironment (USP-TIME) framework are presented as hypotheses for investigating treatment-associated adaptation and the relationship between USP profiles and immune architecture, respectively. Therapeutic development must account for the possibility that USP inhibition also impairs antigen presentation or effector lymphocyte function.

C 补充信息学术文献 · 综述C supplementaryAcademic literature · Review

致癌病毒的神经并发症:机制、临床表现和新见解。

Neurological Complications of Oncogenic Viruses: Mechanisms, Clinical Manifestations, and Emerging Insights.
作者： Lei Liu
摘要中文翻译致癌病毒因其在恶性转化中的关键作用而被广泛认可。然而,它们引起神经系统疾病的能力相对较少受到关注。越来越多的证据表明,多种致癌病毒,包括人类 T 细胞白血病病毒 1 型 (HTLV-1)、人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV)、乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV)、丙型肝炎病毒 (HCV) 和卡波西肉瘤相关疱疹病毒 (KSHV),与涉及中枢和周围神经系统的广泛神经系统并发症有关。这些表现是通过多种机制产生的,包括直接病毒神经侵袭、持续感染、免疫介导的组织损伤、慢性神经炎症和神经血管完整性破坏。此外,病毒诱导的分子改变,如氧化应激、线粒体功能障碍、表观遗传重塑和异常的细胞因子信号传导,会导致神经元功能障碍和疾病进展。在这篇综述中,我们通过整合分子、实验和临床研究的证据,批判性地研究了目前对与主要致癌病毒相关的神经并发症的理解。我们首先讨论病毒感染引起神经损伤的一般机制,强调嗜神经病毒和非嗜神经病毒之间共享的途径。然后,我们总结了与每种致癌病毒相关的神经学表现和病毒特异性致病机制,强调神经发病机制中常见和独特的分子途径。最后,我们解决当前的诊断挑战、新兴生物标志物和治疗策略,同时确定限制因果解释和临床转化的关键知识差距。全面了解致癌病毒、免疫系统和神经系统之间复杂的相互作用将有助于早期 诊断,改善患者管理,并支持开发针对病毒相关神经系统疾病的靶向治疗方法。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注与 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 相关的神经系统并发症,特别研究这些病毒引起神经损伤的机制、其疾病特异性表现以及对 诊断 和 治疗 的影响。
证据基础与方法
该综述整合了分子、实验和临床研究的证据,以严格检查 HPV 的神经系统并发症。它讨论了病毒引起的神经损伤的一般机制,然后总结了病毒特异性的致病机制,强调了常见和独特的分子途径。
共识与结论
该综述的结论是,HPV 可通过多种机制诱发神经系统并发症,包括直接神经侵袭、持续感染、免疫介导的组织损伤、慢性神经炎症和神经血管完整性破坏。这些机制与其他嗜神经病毒和非嗜神经病毒共有。
争议与缺口
该综述确定了限制因果解释和临床转化的关键知识差距,特别是在理解精确的分子途径和特定 HPV 类型在神经系统疾病进展中的作用方面。
科研与实践启示
该综述强调需要进一步研究 HPV、免疫系统和神经系统之间复杂的相互作用,以改进诊断方法、开发有针对性的治疗方法并加强患者管理策略。

Neurological Complications of Oncogenic Viruses: Mechanisms, Clinical Manifestations, and Emerging Insights.

Authors: Lei Liu
Original abstract (English)
Oncogenic viruses are widely recognized for their pivotal role in malignant transformation; however, their capacity to induce neurological disorders has received comparatively less attention. Increasing evidence indicates that several oncogenic viruses, including human T-cell leukemia virus type 1 (HTLV-1), human papillomavirus (HPV), hepatitis B virus (HBV), hepatitis C virus (HCV), and Kaposi's sarcoma-associated herpesvirus (KSHV), are associated with a broad spectrum of neurological complications involving both the central and peripheral nervous systems. These manifestations arise through diverse mechanisms, including direct viral neuroinvasion, persistent infection, immune-mediated tissue injury, chronic neuroinflammation, and disruption of neurovascular integrity. Moreover, virus-induced molecular alterations, such as oxidative stress, mitochondrial dysfunction, epigenetic remodelling, and aberrant cytokine signalling, contribute t neuronal dysfunction and disease progression. In this review, we critically examine the current understanding of the neurological complications associated with major oncogenic viruses by integrating evidence from molecular, experimental, and clinical studies. We first discuss the general mechanisms by which viral infections induce neurological injury, highlighting pathways shared among neurotropic and non-neurotropic viruses. We then summarise the neurological manifestations and virus-specific pathogenic mechanisms associated with each oncogenic virus, emphasising both common and distinct molecular pathways underlying neuropathogenesis. Finally, we address current diagnostic challenges, emerging biomarkers, and therapeutic strategies while identifying critical knowledge gaps that limit causal interpretation and clinical translation. A comprehensive understanding of the complex interactions between oncogenic viruses, the immune system, and the nervous system will facilitate earlier diagnosis, improve patient management, and support the development of targeted therapeutic approaches for virus-associated neurological disorders.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on the neurological complications associated with human papillomaviruses (HPV), specifically examining the mechanisms by which these viruses induce neurological injury, their disease-specific manifestations, and the implications for diagnosis and treatment.
Evidence base and method
The review integrates evidence from molecular, experimental, and clinical studies to critically examine the neurological complications of HPV. It discusses general mechanisms of viral-induced neurological injury and then summarizes virus-specific pathogenic mechanisms, emphasizing both common and distinct molecular pathways.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that HPV can induce neurological complications through various mechanisms, including direct neuroinvasion, persistent infection, immune-mediated tissue injury, chronic neuroinflammation, and disruption of neurovascular integrity. These mechanisms are shared with other neurotropic and non-neurotropic viruses.
Controversies and gaps
The review identifies critical knowledge gaps that limit causal interpretation and clinical translation, particularly in understanding the precise molecular pathways and the role of specific HPV types in neurological disease progression.
Research and practice implications
The review highlights the need for further research into the complex interactions between HPV, the immune system, and the nervous system to improve diagnostic methods, develop targeted therapeutic approaches, and enhance patient management strategies.

了解男性 HPV:患病率、危险因素、健康结果、传播、诊断和 疫苗接种 策略的回顾

Understanding HPV in Men: A Review of the Prevalence, Risk Factors, Health Outcomes, Transmission, Diagnostics, and Vaccination Strategies
作者： Meri Hristamyan
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
Human papillomavirus (HPV) is the most common sexually transmitted viral infection worldwide, with an estimated 630 million people currently infected, and poses significant health risks for men. The aim is to synthesize existing research on HPV in men to better understand its prevalence, risk factors, associated health outcomes, transmission dynamics, diagnostics, and vaccination strategies.
证据基础与方法
我们利用 2010 年至 2025 年期间发表的相关研究进行了非系统性叙述审查,这些研究是通过多个数据库(Web of Science、PubMed、Scopus 和 Google Scholar)和官方健康/疫苗 网站确定的。
共识与结论
全球约三分之一的男性携带生殖器 HPV,约五分之一的男性携带一种或多种 高危型HPV 型。主要危险因素包括性行为、包皮环切状况、安全套使用、艾滋病毒、吸烟、教育程度较低和 疫苗接种 状况。疫苗接种 在男性中显示出很高的疗效,特别是在暴露前给药时,支持针对所有性别扩大 疫苗接种 计划。然而,全球 HPV 疫苗接种 在男性中的覆盖率仍然不理想。
争议与缺口
患病率 在年轻男性中达到顶峰,但在所有年龄段都保持较高水平。超过 80% 的感染者没有症状,并且由于缺乏建议的筛查,许多男性仍然不知情。新兴策略侧重于高危人群,使用肛门巴氏涂片和 HPV DNA 检测。提高采用率的策略包括提供者推荐、教育、量身定制的活动、学校计划、提醒系统、降低成本、追赶 疫苗接种 和开放沟通。
科研与实践启示
扩大针对男性的预防对于减轻 HPV 相关疾病的全球负担至关重要。

Understanding HPV in Men: A Review of the Prevalence, Risk Factors, Health Outcomes, Transmission, Diagnostics, and Vaccination Strategies

Authors: Meri Hristamyan
Original abstract (English)
Background/Objectives: Human papillomavirus (HPV) is the most common sexually transmitted viral infection worldwide, with an estimated 630 million people currently infected, and poses significant health risks for men. While many will naturally clear the infection within a couple of years, persistent cancerous HPV strains are linked to several oncological diseases. The aim is to synthesize existing research on HPV in men to better understand its prevalence, risk factors, associated health outcomes, transmission dynamics, diagnostics, and vaccination strategies. Methods: A non-systematic narrative review was conducted, utilizing relevant studies published between 2010 and 2025 identified using multiple databases (Web of Science, PubMed, Scopus, and Google Scholar) and official health/vaccine websites. Results: Approximately one-third of men globally carry genital HPV, and approximately one-fifth carry one or more high-risk HPV types. Prevalence peaks in younger men but remains high throughout all ages. Key risk factors include sexual behavior, circumcision status, condom use, HIV, smoking, lower education, and vaccination status. More than 80% of infections are asymptomatic, and many men remain unaware due to lack of recommended screening. Emerging strategies focus on high-risk groups, using anal Pap smears and HPV DNA testing. Vaccination in males shows high efficacy, especially when administered before exposure, supporting expanded vaccination programs for all genders. However, global HPV vaccination coverage in men remains suboptimal. Strategies to improve uptake include provider recommendations, education, tailored campaigns, school programs, reminder systems, cost reduction, catch-up vaccination, and open communication. Conclusions: Expanding male-targeted prevention is critical to reducing the global burden of HPV-related diseases.

Consensus and conclusions

Key risk factors include sexual behavior, circumcision status, condom use, HIV, smoking, lower education, and vaccination status. Vaccination in males shows high efficacy, especially when administered before exposure, supporting expanded vaccination programs for all genders. Controversies and gaps

Emerging strategies focus on high-risk groups, using anal Pap smears and HPV DNA testing. Strategies to improve uptake include provider recommendations, education, tailored campaigns, school programs, reminder systems, cost reduction, catch-up vaccination, and open communication.

Expanding male-targeted prevention is critical to reducing the global burden of HPV-related diseases.

C 补充信息学术文献 · 综述C supplementaryAcademic literature · Review

肿瘤病毒对 FAK-Src 信号轴的调节:分子机制、功能后果和治疗机会。

Modulation of the FAK-Src Signalling Axis by Oncoviruses: Molecular Mechanisms, Functional Consequences, and Therapeutic Opportunities.
作者： Yasmeen A Albalawi, Afnan O Deebani, Horeyah Mohammed Almadani, Bian K Almessiry, Zuhair M Mohammedsaleh, Aaishah M H Kaabi, Ahmed M Abdulfattah, Rasha Assad Assiri
摘要中文翻译肿瘤病毒通过操纵 宿主 信号通路(例如 FAK-Src 轴)在细胞转化和癌症进展中发挥作用。临床前证据表明,病毒蛋白可以通过直接相互作用、整合素信号传导、肿瘤抑制因子失活、转录和激酶重编程、钙/ROS信号传导、磷酸酶调节和表观遗传修饰来调节该轴。重要的例子包括 HPV E2 和 E6/E7、EBV LMP1 和 LMP2A、HBV HBx、HCV Core 和 E2 以及 KSHV 蛋白。FAK-Src 破坏可以激活 PI3K/AKT、Rho GTPase、AP-1 和 NF-κB 等途径,从而增强 免疫应答 的迁移、侵袭、存活、血管生成和调节。在某些感染中,该轴也可能参与病毒生命周期的不同阶段。本综述探讨了肿瘤病毒调节 FAK-Src 的分子机制及其功能和治疗意义。它还回顾了 FAK/Src 靶向策略,区分实验抑制剂、多靶点药物和临床评估下的化合物,强调每种病毒特有的有限临床证据。最后,回顾了 pFAK 和 pSrc 作为途径激活和与靶标药效相互作用的潜在标志物;然而,目前的证据不足以将其用作有效的诊断或预测生物标志物。更好地了解病毒-宿主-FAK/Src 相互作用有助于开发基于机制的治疗方法和基于生物标志物的患者选择。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 调节 FAK-Src 信号轴的分子机制、其在 HPV 相关疾病中的功能后果以及治疗机会。核心问题是 HPV 如何操纵 宿主 通路来驱动细胞转化和癌症进展。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对临床前证据的系统分析,包括对 HPV E2 和 E6/E7 等病毒蛋白及其与 FAK-Src 轴相互作用的研究。它概述了用于研究这些相互作用的方法,例如直接蛋白质结合、整合素信号传导和表观遗传修饰。该综述不报告临床试验或汇总估计,但强调研究结果的实验​​性质。
共识与结论
该综述得出的结论是,HPV 蛋白通过多种机制调节 FAK-Src 轴,包括肿瘤抑制因子失活、转录重编程和磷酸酶调节。这些相互作用有助于增强 HPV 相关癌症的迁移、侵袭、存活和免疫调节。
争议与缺口
证据仅限于临床前研究,并且没有足够的数据来确定 FAK-Src 调节在 HPV 感染中的临床相关性。此外,该评论指出,FAK-Src 轴在病毒生命周期不同阶段的作用仍不清楚。
科研与实践启示
该综述强调需要进一步研究 FAK-Src 轴在 HPV 相关疾病中的具体作用以及开发靶向治疗。它还表明,了解病毒-宿主 相互作用可能会导致更好的基于生物标志物的患者选择策略。

Modulation of the FAK-Src Signalling Axis by Oncoviruses: Molecular Mechanisms, Functional Consequences, and Therapeutic Opportunities.

Authors: Yasmeen A Albalawi, Afnan O Deebani, Horeyah Mohammed Almadani, Bian K Almessiry, Zuhair M Mohammedsaleh, Aaishah M H Kaabi, Ahmed M Abdulfattah, Rasha Assad Assiri
Original abstract (English)
Oncoviruses play a role in cellular transformation and cancer progression by manipulating host signalling pathways, such as the FAK-Src axis. Preclinical evidence suggests that viral proteins can modulate this axis through direct interactions, integrin signalling, tumour suppressor inactivation, transcriptional and kinase reprogramming, calcium/ROS signalling, regulation of phosphatases, and epigenetic modifications. Important examples include HPV E2 and E6/E7, EBV LMP1 and LMP2A, HBV HBx, HCV Core and E2, and KSHV proteins. FAK-Src disruption can activate pathways such as PI3K/AKT, Rho GTPase, AP-1, and NF-κB, thereby enhancing migration, invasion, survival, angiogenesis, and modulation of the immune response. In some infections, this axis may also be involved at different stages of the viral life cycle. This review examines the molecular mechanisms of FAK-Src regulation by oncoviruses and its functional and therapeutic implications. It also reviews FAK/Src targeting strategies, distinguishing experimental inhibitors, multitargeted agents, and compounds under clinical evaluation, emphasising the limited clinical evidence specific to each virus. Finally, pFAK and pSrc are reviewed as potential markers of pathway activation and pharmacodynamic interaction with the target; however, current evidence is insufficient for their use as valid diagnostic or predictive biomarkers. A better understanding of the virus-host-FAK/Src interaction could help develop mechanism-based therapeutic approaches and biomarker-based patient selection.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on the molecular mechanisms by which human papillomaviruses (HPV) modulate the FAK-Src signalling axis, its functional consequences in HPV-associated diseases, and therapeutic opportunities. The central question is how HPV manipulates host pathways to drive cellular transformation and cancer progression.
Evidence base and method
The review is a systematic analysis of preclinical evidence, including studies on viral proteins such as HPV E2 and E6/E7, and their interactions with the FAK-Src axis. It outlines the methods used to investigate these interactions, such as direct protein binding, integrin signalling, and epigenetic modifications. The review does not report on clinical trials or pooled estimates but emphasizes the experimental nature of the findings.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that HPV proteins modulate the FAK-Src axis through various mechanisms, including inactivation of tumour suppressors, transcriptional reprogramming, and regulation of phosphatases. These interactions contribute to enhanced migration, invasion, survival, and immune modulation in HPV-associated cancers.
Controversies and gaps
The evidence is limited to preclinical studies, and there is insufficient data to determine the clinical relevance of FAK-Src modulation in HPV infections. Additionally, the review notes that the role of the FAK-Src axis in different stages of the viral life cycle remains unclear.
Research and practice implications
The review highlights the need for further research into the specific roles of the FAK-Src axis in HPV-associated diseases and the development of targeted therapies. It also suggests that understanding virus-host interactions could lead to better biomarker-based patient selection strategies.
